# Mutation-Style Invariant Verification

Every test that claims to protect an invariant must be **demonstrated to fail when that invariant
is deliberately broken**, and the demonstration must be recorded here.

Established by `P0-TSK-038`. This is not a new practice — it is what every task in Phase 0 has
already been doing — so the task is to make it *repeatable and checkable* rather than to introduce
it.

**Why it is a rule and not a habit.** `DEFINITION_OF_DONE.md` §3 lists, among the things that block
done: *"a test would still pass if the invariant it claims to protect were removed"*.
`PHASE_GATES.md` §3 criterion 3 requires that every in-scope `INV-*` have *"at least one test that
fails if the invariant is broken"*. Neither is satisfiable by inspection: a test that cannot fail
looks exactly like a test that passes, and it is worse than no test, because it is believed. This
phase has now found four of them — `secretsAreWrapped`, two rules in `P0-TSK-041`, and a
clock-skew test named for a property it did not exercise.

---

## 1. What counts as a demonstration

A demonstration has three parts, and all three must be recorded:

1. **The mutation** — the specific change that breaks the invariant, precise enough to re-apply.
2. **The command** — what to run.
3. **The observed result** — which tests failed, by name or by count.

"I checked it" is not a demonstration. Neither is "the test asserts X", which is a statement about
the test rather than about what happens when the property is gone.

### Two admissible forms

| Form | What it is | Strength |
|---|---|---|
| **In-suite** | The proof lives in the suite and runs on every build: a fixture that violates the rule, asserted to be rejected | **Strongest.** Re-run continuously, so it cannot rot |
| **Recorded** | The mutation cannot live in the suite — it drops a constraint, widens a grant, or changes production code — so it is a written procedure with its observed result | Weaker: it is re-runnable but only if someone runs it |

**Prefer in-suite.** Use a recorded procedure only where the mutation is one the suite cannot
contain, and the register says which form each demonstration takes so the difference stays visible.

**A recorded demonstration is not prose.** Prose says "we proved it fails". A recorded
demonstration names the mutation, the command and the result, so a reviewer at the phase gate can
re-run it rather than trust it.

### What the form tells you

An in-suite demonstration proves the rule has teeth *today, on this build*. A recorded one proves
it had teeth *on the day it was written*. That difference is the reason the register exists: at the
Phase 0 exit gate, criterion 3 asks a question about now, and only the first form answers it
without work.

---

## 2. The register — invariants of every phase reached

Every invariant `FINANCIAL_INVARIANTS.md` marks as belonging to a phase this project has reached.
`MutationDemonstrationTest` fails the build if one is missing, or if a row names a test class or
method that does not exist.

**The current phase is derived from `CURRENT_STATE.md` rather than written here** (`P1-TSK-024`), so
Phase 2 needs no change to the guard. It enforced *Phase 0 only* until that task, which left the
`INV-IDN` group in exactly the weaker regime the Phase 0 → 1 transition created it to escape.

**A row may name several tests**, and every one of them is checked. The parser required exactly one
until `P1-TSK-024`, and **nine rows therefore did not parse at all** — eight written during Phase 1 —
so the guard was silently not checking that the tests they name exist.

| Invariant | Demonstrated by | Form | The mutation | Observed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INV-MON-01` | `NoFloatingPointMoneyRulesTest#rulesRejectTheirViolations` | In-suite | Four fixtures, one per rule: a `double` field, a `float` signature, a floating-point call target, a floating-point field access | Rejected on every build. Also proven end to end (`P0-TSK-008`) by a `double` planted in `Money`, a `float` in a signature and a `Double.parseDouble` call, each in a different module |
| `INV-MON-01` | `NoFloatingPointMoneyRulesTest#recursiveBoundsDoNotOverflowTheWalk`, `NoFloatingPointMoneyRulesTest#recursiveBoundsDoNotHideFloatingPoint` | In-suite | **The rule's own walk, not the invariant's violation** (test-only fix, 2026-09-30): a type variable whose bound names itself (`E extends Enum<E>`, `T extends Comparable<T>`) sent the generic-signature walk round the cycle forever, so a `P8-TSK-014` helper declaring `<E extends Enum<E>>` crashed the suite with `StackOverflowError` - neither a pass nor a failure. The fix walks each type variable once. Fixtures: exact code with four recursive shapes (the helper's signature verbatim, a self-typed class bound reached through a field, `T extends Comparable<T>`, a two-variable cycle) that all four rules must pass; and three violations reached ONLY through such a bound - a `Double` in the bound after the recursing one, a `float[]` beside the self-reference inside the recursing bound, a class-level bound carrying a `Double` through a field | Red first: before the fix both tests failed with `StackOverflowError` at `isFloatingPoint`. Two wrong fixes, each applied alone and restored byte-identical - stopping at every type variable, and abandoning a variable on meeting its cycle instead of skipping only the repeat - each terminated (the clean probe green) and went blind: `recursiveBoundsDoNotHideFloatingPoint` failed naming `DoubleBehindARecursiveBound`. Verdicts unchanged: a throwaway comparison of the old and new walks over every field, parameter and return type of 1,452 production classes (26,415 types, 482 through a type variable, the exempt classes included) differed nowhere. `./gradlew :app:test --tests "*NoFloatingPointMoneyRulesTest"` |

| `INV-AUD-02` | `MetricConventionTest#tagsCannotCarryUnboundedValues`, `MetricConventionTest#theFragmentExemptionIsBoundedByTheAllowList`, `PaymentMetersTest#theTimerTagsAreBoundedAndTwo` | In-suite | **The FOURTH enforcement site, and the register's own gap**: the invariant's three rows above govern what a type stores, what the platform propagates and what a credential prints - none of them can see a METRIC TAG, which is the disclosure that outlives its request by months in a system with different access control. `P1-TSK-029` built the guard and recorded no row; `P5-TSK-017` widened the tag vocabulary (`provider`, `operation`) and added an escape hatch, which is when an unrecorded guard stops being acceptable (the `P1-TSK-024` finding, repeating). Mutations: a tag key a request could influence added to the provider timer (`payment_id`); separately the fragment exemption pointed at a key that is NOT in `ALLOWED_TAG_KEYS` | Both caught by the intended assertion. The first is the shape that matters - one identifier in a tag turns one series into as many series as there are payments, and the failure is the metrics backend falling over during the incident it was meant to show. The second is why the exemption is not a hole: `provider` is exempt from the SPELLING rule (*prov-**id**-er*) and from nothing else, and a key that nobody argued for in `ALLOWED_TAG_KEYS` cannot borrow that exemption. **Recorded limit**: the fragment rule is a substring match over a key NAME, so it can only ever be a second net - what stops an identifier reaching a tag VALUE is the closed key set plus review, and the `P5-TSK-017` counters carry no value a caller supplies at all |

Three layers, and the raw-SQL probe is what holds the schema's own rank for writers that never run our code. **The trigger itself is NOT mutation-demonstrated, and this row says so rather than implying it**: `P5-TSK-008` built the schema and recorded no sweep, and mutating a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger means editing an applied migration, which only a from-scratch database re-runs (the `P2-TSK-017` grant-mutation precedent). What IS demonstrated is that the trigger is present and effective for every writer - raw SQL bypassing the command is refused with `payments_refund_is_bounded` - which is the property the invariant needs; the missing half is whether the trigger's own predicate is right, and it is owed to the phase audit |

The moved check keeps that precondition green and fails the **outcome half**: the loser blocks, resumes, judges its pre-lock snapshot and wrongly accepts - two holds of 600 in 1000 - and the ten-way race admits more than was available. Intact, ten instances placing 1000 against 3000 accept exactly three, counted in the table, with `holds_minor` equal to the fold of the active rows |

The load-bearing half is the **empty** entry: a zero-line entry balances vacuously — every currency's two sums are equal at zero — so a balance check alone waves it through, which is why the line count is its own check at the domain and its own trigger at the schema (a line trigger never fires for an entry with no lines) |

The demonstration carries its own positive control: the migrator — the role the grant does not bind — corrects an **unposted** account's classification, a journal line lands, and the same coherent update is refused with `check_violation`. So the freeze binds exactly when the invariant says it must, for every writer |

| `INV-AUD-04` | `AdjustmentEndpointDatabaseTest#selfApprovalIsRefused`, `AdjustmentEndpointDatabaseTest#anOperatorPostsAnAdjustment`, `AdjustmentEndpointDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentApprovalsProduceOneEntry`, `AdjustmentProposalSchemaDatabaseTest#aRawSqlAdjustmentCannotCommitUnapproved`, `AdjustmentProposalSchemaDatabaseTest#aSelfApprovedProposalRowIsUnstorable`, `AdjustmentProposalSchemaDatabaseTest#theProposalIsFrozenForEveryWriter`, `AdjustmentProposalTest#selfApprovalIsRefusedByTheAggregate` | Recorded | The domain's self-approval check dropped from `AdjustmentProposal.requireApprovableBy` (`V010`'s CHECK then fires as our 500 where the invariant owes an actionable 409); `V010`'s `approver <> initiator` CHECK dropped; the deferred `adjustment_entry_is_approved` trigger dropped (a raw-SQL adjustment commits unapproved); the approval's `FOR UPDATE` read made a plain read (the ten-way race loses its serialisation point); the payload freeze trigger dropped; the `ledger.AdjustmentProposed` audit dropped from the propose effect; the posted entry's actor made the *initiator* instead of the approver (`P3-TSK-021`) | All caught by the intended assertion. The catalogue's Verify line — *self-approval rejection tests* — finally has its subject: the refusal writes **nothing**, and a second person approves the same proposal as the positive control. Its observed shape is worth naming rather than assuming: the losers do not commit a second transfer, they abort with `IdempotencyStorageException` (*the claim was no longer in progress when its outcome was recorded*) - so a retry that should have replayed reaches the customer as a 500, and the effect count survives only because those transactions roll back. The enumeration is not prose: `permittedTransitions()`, `sqlValueList()` and `sqlTerminalValueList()` generate `V002`'s status `CHECK`s on all three columns and the transition trigger's edge conditions, and `TransferMigrationTest` reconciles all four generated artefacts, so a state added in code without its constraint - or a constraint widened without the code - fails the build hermetically |

| `INV-IDN-01` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aReplayedCreateRendersNoToken`, `#tenConcurrentCreatesProduceOneSession` | In-suite | The idempotency claim taught to record the creation's RESPONSE (the byte-for-byte replay discipline every other keyed command follows) instead of the session id alone, and to re-show it on a replay | Caught at both ranks: the replay rendered a live checkout token, and the race counted ten answers carrying one where exactly one may. | `INV-IDN-01` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#theTokenIsStoredNowhere` | In-suite | The claim STORES the token beside the session id while the replay still renders none - the silent half of the mutation above, invisible to any assertion about a response | Caught, naming the column: `platform.idempotency_record.response_body`. TWO GATE FINDINGS ARE FOLDED INTO THIS ROW. First, the sweep had been the LAST assertion of the replay test, so under the mutation above it never executed - a demonstration that cannot run is not a demonstration, and it is now its own test. Second, the sweep could not have seen this column anyway: `bytea::text` renders `\x7365...`, so a `LIKE` over a printable needle never matched, and the one column a credential most plausibly leaks into is exactly that type. Now cast with `encode(col, 'escape')` - here, in `MerchantApiKeyDatabaseTest` and in `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest`, all three of which shared the blindness |
| `INV-MER-01` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aMerchantReadsOnlyItsOwnSession`, `OwnershipIsScopedTest` | In-suite | The `merchant_ref = ?` predicate neutralised in the statement (the parameter still bound, so the SQL still looks scoped) | Caught at TWO RANKS - behaviourally, a merchant read a competitor's session including its line summary; and by the build rule, which is the sharper half because it fails without anybody writing a test for the NEXT tenant-scoped read. The second rank is a gate finding: the predicate had been a Java `filter` over an unscoped `findById`, correct today and invisible to the register tomorrow, and it moved into the statement so this mutation could be caught at all. The negative is a real second tenant with a real session of its own (`P6-TSK-004`'s survivor lesson, applied while writing the test rather than after it) |
| `INV-IDEM-01` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aRetriedConfirmationConverges`, `#aStrangerCannotConvergeOnSomebodyElsesPurchase` | In-suite | (a) the `isPaid()` convergence branch removed from `CheckoutService.openPayment`, (b) its ownership re-establishment removed | Caught both times. (a) is the defect this suite FOUND: a retried confirmation of a purchase that SUCCEEDED answered `409 checkout.NotConfirmable`, to a customer who holds only a token and has no read surface to ask. (b) is the risk the fix creates and must not: a second holder of the token learned the payer's intent and order |
| `INV-MER-06` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aCaptureLandingAfterExpiryStillProducesTheOrder` | In-suite | `CheckoutSessions.completionOf`'s `EXPIRED -> COMPLETED_LATE` arm removed, so a capture landing on an expired offer completes nothing | Caught - the session stayed `EXPIRED`, no order existed and the merchant's payable stayed at zero while the money had moved. THE ROW THIS INVARIANT WAITED SIX PHASES FOR: the race is driven the way production produces it (the capture's provider response LOST, the sweeper expiring the offer, the payments sweeper then resolving through the same `PaymentOutcomes` every resolver shares), and the assertion is the derived payable position - the same 96.80 the timely ordering pays |
| `INV-MER-04` | `ReturnedFeeTest#aOneCentFeeSplitInHalfDoesNotDouble`, `#everySequenceTelescopes`, `MerchantCaptureDatabaseTest#twoPartialRefundsReturnExactlyTheAssessedFee` | In-suite | `FeeCalculation.returnedFee` reverted to the NAIVE per-refund share - `round(fee x r / gross)` once per refund instead of differencing a cumulative allocation | Caught at BOTH RANKS, and the arithmetic rank is dramatic: a ONE-CENT fee refunded in two halves returns TWO cents naively, because each half's share is exactly half a cent and `HALF_UP` rounds both up - the platform paying out twice what it ever charged. The 1800-case sweep (amounts x rates x six policies x 0/2/3-minor currencies) fails too, and the database test now uses that same discriminating split: ITS FIRST DRAFT USED 33.33/66.67 OF AN ODD FEE, WHICH THE NAIVE FORMULA HAPPENS TO GET RIGHT, so the probe survived the database rank until the split was chosen to discriminate rather than merely to look uneven |

Closed by making the race rather than waiting for it - the view's connection is wrapped so that the instant its line read returns, another connection commits a posting to the same payable; any later statement sees it and the line read does not. The probe restores exactly that gap, which is what makes this row the closure of that finding rather than a new assertion beside it |
| `INV-MER-04` | `PaymentsSchemaDatabaseTest#theCompletedSumIsNotTheBudgetSum` | In-suite | `PaymentOutcomes.applyRefund` reading `sumNonFailedFor` (the budget bound) instead of `sumCompletedFor` when it computes `refundedBefore` | **SURVIVED, and the account is honest rather than patched.** The two sums are PROVEN to differ - a refund in flight counts toward the budget and not toward what has left - but nothing drives `applyRefund` with a sibling refund still in flight, so the CALLER'S CHOICE between them is unbound. What the analysis does establish is that the failure mode is LOUD rather than silent: an inflated `refundedBefore` pushes the cumulative total past the capture and `returnedFee` throws `exceed`, failing the refund transaction, instead of quietly returning a wrong share. The interleaving that binds it arrives with `P6-TST-002`'s storm, which runs concurrent refunds on one merchant; recorded here so that task inherits a question rather than discovering one |
| `INV-MER-06` | `CheckoutExpiryDatabaseTest#anOverduePendingSessionExpires` | In-suite | `PAYMENT_PENDING` removed from `findExpirable`'s candidate query | Caught. The edge is not decoration: ADR-0053 gave checkout no failure state on purpose, so a customer whose card declines and who then closes the tab leaves a `PAYMENT_PENDING` row that NOTHING ELSE CAN EVER END. This is its only terminal escape, and without it ADR-0044's every-state-has-a-producer doctrine is broken by the state that most needs it |
| `INV-MER-06` | `CheckoutExpiryDatabaseTest#aSweeperRacingAConfirmationHasOneWinner` | In-suite | `CheckoutExpirySweeper.isOverdue` reverted to `hasExpired(clock)` alone - the grace applied at the QUERY and not at the DECISION | Caught, and THE MUTATION IS THE DEFECT THE SUITE FOUND. A row read as `OPEN` in the candidate list and confirmed by a customer before the sweeper takes its lock was expired as `PAYMENT_PENDING` with a payment one second old, because the grace only ever applied to rows selected AS pending. Harmless to the money (the late edge still catches the capture) and corrosive to the meaning: `completed_late` would count confirmations that raced a tick rather than providers that answered late |
| `INV-CON-02` | `CheckoutExpiryDatabaseTest#tenSweepersProduceOneExpiry` | In-suite | Two forms. (a) the conditional transition's row count IGNORED, so every racer audits and announces; (b) the state check under the lock removed | (a) SURVIVED, and the reason is recorded rather than patched: the `FOR UPDATE` lock serializes every racer, so a loser re-reads `EXPIRED`, finds `canTransitionTo(EXPIRED)` false and returns before the row count can matter - the `P6-TSK-003` shape exactly. The clause still binds a caller that reads WITHOUT the lock, and `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#...` already drives that stale-snapshot path directly. (b) is the sharper form and was CAUGHT: nine racers each tried to expire an already-expired row and the aggregate threw, so the tick reported a failed row |
| `INV-MER-01` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#theWithdrawalCommandIsTenantScoped` | In-suite | `CheckoutSessions.abandon`'s `findOwnedBy` weakened to an unscoped `findById` | SURVIVED FIRST, WHICH IS THE BATTERY WORKING, and the gap was closed where it was found. Over HTTP a stranger already got `404` and the row stayed `OPEN` - but BOTH came from the RENDER, which is tenant-scoped and shares the command's transaction, so its refusal rolled the withdrawal back. The predicate at the write was unreachable by any behavioural test, and a predicate no test can reach is one a later refactor removes (render outside the transaction, or answer `204`, and cross-tenant withdrawal is live). The command is now driven directly and the mutation then failed against it |
| `INV-AUD-03` | `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aMerchantWithdrawsItsOwnOffer`, `#anExpiredSessionRefusesAFreshConfirmation` | In-suite | (a) the `ABANDONED` convergence removed from `CheckoutSessions.abandon`; (b) the `EXPIRED` arm removed from `CheckoutService.openPayment`, so the state falls through to the generic refusal | Caught both times. (b) is a `P6-TSK-008` gate finding in its own right: the STATE and the CLOCK were telling the same customer about the same dead offer different things - `SessionExpired` before the sweeper ran and `NotConfirmable` after - so the answer depended on whether a background job had ticked |
| `INV-PAY-03` | `MerchantCaptureDatabaseTest#theMerchantBoundCapturePostsTheFourLines`, `#aWalletTopUpIsUnchanged` | In-suite | The composition seam itself (`P6-TSK-005`, ADR-0050 section 6). | `INV-EVT-01` | `MerchantCaptureDatabaseTest#theAssessmentIsAnnouncedWithTheEntry` | In-suite | `merchant.FeeAssessed` in the capture's own transaction (`P6-TSK-005`). Mutation: the announcement dropped | Caught twice - the announcement test, and the ten-way race's announced-once assertion. The event carries the pinned version id, so a consumer can reproduce the fee from the version alone, which is `INV-HIST-04` on the wire |
| `INV-MER-01` | `FeeScheduleDatabaseTest#pricingIsResolvedPerTenant` | In-suite | The tenant predicate on the pricing resolution (`P6-TSK-004`). Mutation: `a.merchant_id = ?` neutralised in `JdbcFeeScheduleStore.findEffectiveVersionFor` | **SURVIVED ITS FIRST RUN, and that is the battery working.** The suite's only negative was that an unassigned merchant resolves EMPTY - which an unscoped query also answers whenever no other assignment happens to be committed at that moment, so the assertion rested on execution order rather than on the predicate. | `INV-MER-06` | `CheckoutSessionTest#expiredIsNotTerminal`, `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#landedMoneyHasADestination`, `CheckoutSessionMigrationTest#landedMoneyHasADestinationInTheSchema` | In-suite | **The invariant's first subject** (`P6-TSK-006`, ADR-0053 section 5). Worth all three: if `EXPIRED` were terminal, a capture landing after the clock ran out would have nowhere to go, and the trigger's refusal would arrive INSIDE the capture's own transaction, after the money had moved. | `INV-LIFE-02` | `CheckoutSessionTest#everyIllegalEdgeIsRefused`, `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#everyIllegalEdgeIsRefusedByTheTrigger` | In-suite | The session machine at the aggregate and at the database (`P6-TSK-006`). | `INV-LIFE-01` | `CheckoutSessionTest#theClockRefusesEvenWhileTheRowSaysOpen` | In-suite | **Expiry gates dispatch** (`P6-TSK-006`, ADR-0053 section 5) - and the design point this row exists to record: `EXPIRED` is a state the SWEEPER earns, never a `WHERE expires_at < now()` filter pretending to be a state, which is what makes it countable and audited; the aggregate ALSO checks the clock, because a sweeper runs on its own cadence and the gap between the deadline and the sweep is a window in which money lands on a dead offer. Mutation: the clock check dropped from `confirm` | Caught - a session past its deadline, still reading `OPEN`, accepted a confirmation. | `INV-IDN-01` | `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#noColumnHoldsTheToken`, `#aTokenCannotOpenTwoSessions` | In-suite | The platform's **fifth** credential value and the narrowest (`P6-TSK-006`): possession grants ONE checkout session. (a) at **two ranks** - the column's shape `CHECK` refuses a base64url plaintext outright, and the sweep across every text column in every schema would catch it if that constraint ever loosened; the needle is reconstructed from randomness the TEST supplied, so proving the property did not require widening `plaintext()` past package-private. | `INV-CON-01` | `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#transitionsAreConditionalAndRecorded` | In-suite | The arbiter ADR-0053 section 5 names, and the only one this phase needs (`P6-TSK-006`): an expiry and a confirmation meet on one row as two conditional transitions and exactly one wins. | `INV-CON-01` | `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#aConflictingIntentIsRefusedRatherThanDiscarded` | In-suite | **A GATE FINDING NO PROBE MADE** (`P6-TSK-006`). Closed with a predicate that refuses the write; the mutation removing it then failed against the new test | Caught once the gap was closed. Recorded because the shape recurs: a guard that makes a write a no-op is not the same as a guard that refuses it |
| `INV-HIST-01` | `CheckoutSessionDatabaseTest#theOfferIsFrozenForEveryWriter`, `#anOrderCannotBeChanged`, `#tenConcurrentOrdersProduceOne` | In-suite | The offer and the fact (`P6-TSK-006`, ADR-0053 sections 1 and 6). | `INV-IDN-01` | `MerchantApiKeySecretTest#nothingRendersTheSecret`, `SecretsAreUnwrappedInOnePlaceTest#theUnwrapSitesAreExactlyTheOnesNamed` | In-suite | The unwrap set, extended to a second module. | `INV-IDN-02` | `MerchantApiKeyMigrationTest#noColumnCouldHoldASecret`, `MerchantApiKeySecretTest#theHashHasTheShapeTheSchemaConstrains` | In-suite | The derivation recorded beside the value, for the platform's fourth credential kind (`P6-TSK-002`): `algorithm` is `NOT NULL` and the hash's shape is `CHECK`-bounded, so a plaintext secret would not fit the column quietly. | `INV-AUD-03` | `MerchantApiKeyDatabaseTest#aRevokedKeyRefusesImmediately` | In-suite | The reason required on a security judgement (`P6-TSK-002`), asserted VERBATIM in the audit record. | `INV-AUD-03` | `DenyByDefaultDatabaseTest#negativeAuthorizationForPrivilegedEndpoints`, `DenyByDefaultDatabaseTest#anUndeclaredEndpointIsRefused` | In-suite | Remove the permission check from the interceptor; separately, remove deny-by-default | Both caught (`P1-TSK-020`). **Also added by `P1-TSK-024`, and it is the row a narrower extension would have missed**: it is `Phase: 1 onward` and is not in the `INV-IDN` group, so extending the guard to `INV-IDN-*` - which is what this task's own text asked for - would have left it out |
| `INV-KYC-01` | `VerificationAdapterTest#unknownStateIsIndeterminate`, `VerificationRunDatabaseTest#aHitBlocks`, `VerificationRunDatabaseTest#evidenceIsAppendOnly` | Recorded | A provider verdict made the decision: the port's unknown-verdict default mapped to `CLEAR` instead of `INDETERMINATE`; a `HIT` allowed past the assessment; the evidence write dropped, so the verdict would be the only record of what the provider said | Each caught by the intended assertion (`P2-TSK-009`). The adapter matrix holds the port **total** - every misbehaviour is a result, never success - a `HIT` never terminates the case (`IN_REVIEW` has no edge to a terminal), and the bytes received are retained verbatim and append-only. No decision exists until the platform's own act: a reviewed case with every verdict in stays undecided (`DecisionDatabaseTest#anUnreviewedCaseIsNotReady`) |
| `INV-KYC-02` | `DecisionDatabaseTest#theDecisionIsImmutableAtThePrivilegeLevel`, `DecisionDatabaseTest#theAutomaticDecisionReplays` | Recorded | `GRANT UPDATE` on the decision tables in `V007`; separately the reviewer's audit written as the platform, and the evidence references dropped | All caught (`P2-TSK-013`), the grant mutation against a **from-scratch** database. There is no `UPDATE` grant at all, so the privilege IS the immutability - the `audit_record` model - with `UPDATE`/`DELETE` proven denied on every column of both tables. The replay test re-derives the stored outcome from the check rows the record references and the pinned policy version - `INV-CRD-01`'s regime, three phases early |
| `INV-KYC-03` | `ProviderCallbackDatabaseTest#aDuplicatedDeliveryHasOneEffect`, `ProviderCallbackDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentDeliveriesProduceOneEffect`, `DecisionDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentDecisionsProduceOne` | Recorded | The inbox dedupe bypassed, the effect run directly; the conditional completion made unconditional, so a late callback reopens a terminal check; the decision's own arbiter (`READY_FOR_DECISION` &rarr; terminal) ignored | Each caught (`P2-TSK-011`, `P2-TSK-013`). Two dedupe layers blind in different directions - the inbox absorbs **identical** deliveries by `deliveryId`, the conditional transition absorbs **distinct** re-sends - and N concurrent deciders produce one decision with N-1 losers told the truth: a triple delivery and a ten-way race each land one completion, one evidence row, one audit record |
| `INV-KYC-04` | `ScreeningRunDatabaseTest#aHitBecomesWorkForAPerson`, `KycDecisionTest#theAutomaticPolicyRefusesANonClearCheck`, `ReviewDatabaseTest#bothEndpointsRefuseASessionWithNoRole` | Recorded | A `HIT` allowed past the assessment; the routing to `IN_REVIEW` removed, so a hit case never becomes work for a person; the automatic policy's non-`CLEAR` predicate removed - an automatic approval of a case that owed a person a judgement | Each caught (`P2-TSK-009`, `P2-TSK-010`, `P2-TSK-013`). Structurally the case machine has no edge from `IN_REVIEW` to a terminal, `ChecksAssessment` decides `HIT` first so not even a later `CLEAR` of the type un-blocks, and the automatic refusal is proven **at the domain** because the `BLOCKED`-first ordering makes it unreachable in production. Resolution requires `KYC_REVIEW` (negative authorization test), a reason, and is audited naming the reviewer |
| `INV-KYC-05` | `CustomerProjectionDatabaseTest#theDecisionAndTheProjectionCannotDrift`, `CustomerProjectionDatabaseTest#aClosedCustomerRefusesTheDecisionWhole`, `PartyModuleIsolationTest#seesNoSiblingAndNoCompositionRoot` | Recorded | The projection dropped from either door; the `DecisionOutcome` &rarr; `CustomerStatus` mapping inverted; the lost-move failure swallowed, so a decision is recorded beside an unmoved customer - the silent drift this invariant forbids | Each caught (`P2-TSK-014`). The reconciliation sweep holds both directions - the sharp one being that **no customer under verification leaves `PENDING` without a decision authorizing it** - a lost projection fails the whole transaction loudly with nothing written, and the module boundary keeps `party` unable to see `kyc`, so nothing outside the orchestration can compute the status |
| `INV-CNS-01` | `ConsentGateDatabaseTest#theCausesOfRefusalAreOneAnswer`, `ConsentEndpointDatabaseTest#absenceAndWithdrawalAreOneAnswer`, `ConsentHistoryDatabaseTest#theDerivationFollowsTheHistory`, `CustomerOpenedOpensCaseTest#aRefusedOpeningWritesNothing` | Recorded | The `GRANT` predicate dropped from the derivation, so a **withdrawal** derives a basis; the gate made to permit everything; the consumer opening without asking the gate at all; separately the query's per-purpose derivation inverted | Each caught by the intended assertion (`P2-TSK-017`, `P2-TSK-018`, `P2-TSK-019`). The consumer mutation is caught hermetically by the store never being asked: an open attempted and converged away would still have been an attempt to process without a basis |
| `INV-CNS-02` | `ConsentHistoryDatabaseTest#theHistoryIsImmutableToTheApplication`, `ConsentHistoryDatabaseTest#textsArriveOnlyByMigration`, `ConsentMigrationTest#recordGrantsAreThePrivilegeModel`, `ConsentMigrationTest#textsAreUnwritableByTheApplication` | Recorded | `GRANT ... UPDATE` added on `consent_record` in `V002`; separately `GRANT ... INSERT` added on `consent_text` | Both caught against a **from-scratch** database (`P2-TSK-017`). There is no `UPDATE` grant at all, so the privilege IS the immutability (the `audit_record` model, no freeze trigger), proven per column from `information_schema` - and the sweep probes `seq` with `= DEFAULT` rather than `= seq`, because `GENERATED ALWAYS` refuses the latter before the privilege check runs, which would have rested the assertion on the wrong refusal |
| `INV-CNS-03` | `ConsentWithdrawalBlocksTheCapabilityDatabaseTest#aWithdrawalOnOneInstanceBlocksCaseOpeningOnAnother`, `ConsentGateDatabaseTest#withdrawalOnOneInstanceRefusesOnAnother`, `NoProcessLocalConsentStateTest#nothingHoldsConsentState` | Recorded | The gate's authoritative read replaced by a cached value, twice: memoised per (party, purpose) on the **gate**, and - one layer down, where the field detector structurally cannot see it - memoised inside `JdbcConsentStore.hasCurrentBasis` | Both caught, at the gate **and at the capability** (`P2-TST-002`) - the milestone bullet names the capability, so the real consumer is driven with the real stores, gate and writers rather than the property being assembled from two green tests. The race holds instance B's transaction open and straddles the **commit**: while the withdrawal is uncommitted A still permits (an instance refusing there would be reading dirty), and on A's very next decision after the commit it refuses. **The detector SURVIVED the store-level cache** - a `Map<String, Boolean>` names no consent type - so the behavioural test is the load-bearing control for that shape and the detector is the second, blind in a different direction |
| `INV-CNS-04` | `ConsentHistoryDatabaseTest#theVersionPinIsUnforgeable`, `ConsentHistoryDatabaseTest#reconsentIsAPropertyOfTheVersion`, `ConsentMigrationTest#theVersionPinIsComposite`, `ConsentEndpointDatabaseTest#aStaleGrantIsRefusedWhenReconsentIsDemanded`, `ConsentGateDatabaseTest#aStaleBasisRefuses` | Recorded | `text_version` made nullable; the composite FK `(purpose, text_version)` dropped; the re-consent clause dropped from the derivation; the boundary's stale-version refusal dropped | Each caught (`P2-TSK-017`, `P2-TSK-018`). The two schema mutations are **independently** load-bearing: SQL's composite-FK semantics skip the check when any column is NULL, so dropping the NOT NULL lets an unpinned record through the FK that is still present - which is why the schema carries both. Whether a version lapses earlier grants is read from the version's own `requires_reconsent`, so the gate's next decision refuses with no restart and no sweep in between |
| `INV-KYC-06` | `DocumentUploadDatabaseTest#theReadPathDecryptsVerifiesAndAudits`, `DocumentUploadDatabaseTest#anUploadStoresEncryptedChecksummedEvidence`, `DocumentCipherTest#tamperIsRefused`, `DocumentCipherTest#wrongKeyIsRefused` | Recorded | The audit write dropped from `DocumentAccess.read`, so content is delivered with no trail of who looked; and the content persisted in the clear, so the ciphertext column holds the plaintext | Both caught, and **performed by this review** rather than inferred (`P2-DOC-001`; the `P0-TSK-038` review's finding that one row's observed column had been reasoned to). The unaudited read is caught by exactly the intended assertion, naming *“the read path decrypts, verifies the checksum, and audits who looked”*; the plaintext storage is caught by the `information_schema` column sweep among four others, because content stored in the clear also fails to decrypt on the way back. | `INV-HIST-02` | `VerificationRunDatabaseTest#aCleanRunReachesReadyForDecision`, `VerificationRunDatabaseTest#evidenceIsAppendOnly`, `ProviderCallbackDatabaseTest#aLateCallbackIsEvidenceOnly` | Recorded | The evidence append dropped from the run path, so a provider's answer is normalised into an outcome and the bytes that produced it are gone | Caught by the intended assertion — *“a clean run takes the case to APPROVED with retained evidence”* — **performed by this review** (`P2-DOC-001`), and by the owning tasks' own sweeps before it (`P2-TSK-009`, `P2-TSK-011`). | `INV-LIFE-03` | `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aFirstSendsRefusedConnectionFailsTheRefund`, `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aTakeoversRefusedConnectionProvesNothing`, `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aFirstSendsRefusedConnectionAfterARenewalProvesNothing` | Recorded | **The Phase 6 → 7 transition's first CRITICAL: a refund's re-send is judged by its send permit** (payments `V009`, ADR-0057 §4 brought to refunds). Three mutations: `PaymentRefund.judged` dropped, so every `NOTHING_SENT` failed the refund; the locked row's permit ignored, so a first send's refused connection failed a refund a later permit had already re-sent; the first-send knowledge dropped, so no refused connection proved anything | All caught, each where it should be: the judgement dropped failed the takeover's and the renewal's tests (the refund `FAILED` and released while a first send may have paid); the row permit ignored failed the renewal test alone; the first-send knowledge dropped failed the first-send test - the one refused connection that proves nothing was ever transmitted left the refund `UNKNOWN` |
| `INV-IDEM-01` | `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aTakeoverFindingAFinishedRefundSendsNothing`, `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aKeyCarriesItsRefundPastTheClaim` | Recorded | The transition's I4: a takeover that finds its refund already finished dispatched a fresh one (the convergence's terminal branch removed); separately, a refund found under the key with DIFFERENT facts dispatched fresh instead of refusing as `RefundKeyReusedException` | Both caught. | `INV-LIFE-03` | `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#theSweepReDrivesARefundTheProviderNeverSaw` | Recorded | The transition's I2, the sweep's refund leg - Phase 5's debt, re-owned by the Phase 6 review and paid here. | `INV-LIFE-03` | `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aStuckDispatchedRefundIsCountedPastTheBound`, `PaymentMetricsDatabaseTest#aDispatchOrAnAuthorizationPastTheBoundCounts` | Recorded | I2's gauges, in `P6-TSK-013`'s payout shape: the refund gauge made blind to `DISPATCHED` (Phase 5's unknown-only reading restored); the attempt gauge made blind to an `AUTHORIZED` past the bound | Both caught, each by its own test: a refund crashed mid-flight, and an authorization nothing captured, read zero - invisible exactly when the sweep is down |
| `INV-LIFE-03` | `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#theSweepChainsAStrandedAuthorization` | Recorded | The transition's I6: the sweeper's stranded-authorization leg removed, restoring Phase 5's one chain - the HTTP surface, after a synchronous `AUTHORIZED` | Caught: the authorization stayed `AUTHORIZED` and nothing captured. | `INV-LIFE-02` | `PaymentsSchemaDatabaseTest#theRefundSendPermitIsForwardOnlyAndOnlyWhileResolvable` | Recorded | `V009`'s two clauses, each removed from the trigger function: the permit's forward-only clause, so a writer could step it back; the resolved-refund clause, so a resolved refund's permit could be renewed and the refund sent again | Both caught by raw-SQL writes as the application role - the schema's rank beneath the command's conditional renewal |
| `INV-BAL-03` | `AccountClosingDatabaseTest#aPaymentInFlightBlocksTheClose`, `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aConfirmationToAClosedWalletDispatchesNothing`, `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aConfirmationRacingACloseWaitsAndRefuses` | Recorded | **The transition's second CRITICAL, C2**: a customer could close the wallet an open top-up credits, and the card was then captured into a posting the ledger refused - the customer charged, the platform's books silent, the payment stuck `CAPTURE_DISPATCHED`.
