| `INV-REC-04` | `ClaimantOrderAndReplayDatabaseTest` - three cases | Recorded | Three `P8-DOC-001` probes: a fingerprint seen in an earlier run ignored; the chunk walking items by id; a rematch's stored judged amount one unit off | Each caught - the cross-file duplicate typed by exhaustion; claimant outcomes changed by row ids; a battery run replayed `DIVERGED` |
| `INV-REC-09` | `AtomicityByFailureInjectionDatabaseTest`#aParkReDrivenOnAMovedClockConverges | Recorded | A `P8-DOC-001` probe: a re-driven park's convergence removed | Caught (the suspense item's unique refused a second park and the leg threw). First aimed at the wrong suite and survived there - no verdict; re-run against this suite, caught |
| `INV-SET-04` | `IntakeAtomicityAndRacesDatabaseTest` - two cases | Recorded | Two `P8-DOC-001` probes: a live-batch conflict no longer answered `CONFLICTING_BATCH` (C2); a losing decliner re-running from a terminal file (C4) | Both caught - nine parsers counted the conflict as their own failure; a racer met an unanswered failure |
| `INV-AUD-04` | `BatchRepudiationDatabaseTest` (the ten-proposer case) | Recorded | A `P8-DOC-001` probe: the one-live-proposal unique's answer dropped (C14) | Caught (losers answered 500, never 409) |
| `INV-SET-07` | `FormatDefectBoundTest` - three cases | Recorded | A `P8-DOC-001` probe: the 100-defect bound off by one in the scheme, payout and bank-statement formats | Caught three ways (101 defects kept) |
| `INV-SET-03` | `LateEvidenceAndBreakTypingDatabaseTest` - two cases | Recorded | Two `P8-DOC-001` probes: the rematch worklist no longer reading PARKED items; the end-to-end reference reaching nothing | Both caught - a chargeback arriving ahead of its dispute stayed PARKED after the stage opened (§14.15); a pay-in's in-flight line typed UNKNOWN_EXTERNAL instead of MISSING_INTERNAL through the real lookup |
| `INV-REC-06` | `SettlementReconciliationStormDatabaseTest` (the at-rest projection verification) | Recorded | A `P8-DOC-001` probe: the projection never applied for a posting | Caught - first by the storm's own HTTP flows failing (a 500) before its at-rest reading was reached, which is recorded as such: the at-rest `ProjectionVerification` is the backstop, the live flows the first rank |
| `INV-SET-07` | `BatchRepudiationDatabaseTest` (the settlement module's every-writer readmission case; the app suite of the same name, case 15, asserts the same rank over HTTP, not run under the probe) | Recorded | A the Phase 8 -> 9 transition re-gate probe (NEW-SEC-1): settlement `V014`'s walk reverted to `V009`'s alone, so a repudiated batch's file passes its authentication on again and one readmitter re-accepts a four-eyes-repudiated recognition | Caught - `settlement.file_inherits_authentication` answered `true` where the suite demands `false`, on exactly the new assertion (the suite's other five cases green); restore byte-identical (sha256 `90168a67bf67e791872898e4b575f29a9b3dff95773c08cd41af9c3f95d80210` before and after) |
| `INV-CRD-02` | `CreditMigrationTest#theOwnerIsRefusedByTheTrigger`, `ReasonCodeCatalogueTest#theEnumAndTheSeedAgreeBothWays` | Recorded | Two `P10-TSK-001` probes, the catalogue element: the reason-code immutability trigger made to `RETURN NULL` instead of refusing (credit `V002`); a `ReasonCode` member added with no row | Each caught - the owner's `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` no longer refused; the member reported as having no row. Restored byte-identical (sha256-verified) |
| `INV-CRD-04` | `CreditVocabularyTest#aDecisionIsNeverAReferral` | Recorded | A `P10-TSK-001` probe: `REFER` added to `DecisionOutcome` | Caught. Restored byte-identical (sha256-verified) |
| `INV-CRD-12` | `NoFloatingPointMoneyRulesTest` | Recorded | A `P10-TSK-001` probe: a `double` field planted in `CreditProduct` - the guard reaching `credit` from its first class | Caught (`noFieldHoldsAFloatingPointValue`). Restored byte-identical (sha256-verified) |

## 3. What the register does not claim

**`INV-AUD-01` is demonstrated only in half.** The registry proves that a *recorded* action is one
the catalogue knows about. It cannot detect a privileged action that writes **no audit record at
all**, and `P0-TSK-023` recorded that limit rather than glossing it: a registry that looked
complete while the calls were missing would be worse than none, because it would be believed. The
missing half needs the Phase 15 audit-completeness verification.

**`INV-AUD-04`'s row could not be written until `P3-TSK-021`, and now exists** — kept here
because the episode is the register's doctrine working end to end. `P3-TST-003` found the
mechanism deliberately unbuilt (no threshold, no second approver, four-eyes recorded as
ADR-0010's debt by `P3-TSK-017`), refused to write a row naming the adjustment suite's
authorization tests — a false row is worse than a missing one, because it is believed — and
recorded instead that the battery would fail naming `INV-AUD-04` at the status flip, since
`MutationDemonstrationTest` derives its demanded set from the catalogue. `P3-TSK-021` built
the mechanism (two authenticated acts: propose, then a second person's approval posting the
entry; approver ≠ initiator at `DOMAIN` and `DB-CONSTRAINT`, the invariant's own Enforce
clause) and landed the row in §2 with the self-approval rejection tests the catalogue's
Verify line names — before `P3-DOC-001`, exactly as scheduled.

**`INV-SET-01`'s Phase-5 demonstration had to be built before its row could be written, and
the gap it exposed was wider than the invariant** (`P5-TST-002`). The catalogue's Verify line
reads *lifecycle tests per rail*, and the lifecycle half existed — `PaymentAttemptTest` pins
`values()` exactly, so a `SETTLED` state cannot be added unnoticed. The *accounting* half did
not: the capture's ADR-0048 pair (DR `SETTLEMENT_CLEARING` / CR wallet) is what makes
"captured is not settled" a fact about money rather than a sentence in a javadoc, and
**pointing the debit at `SUSPENSE_UNMATCHED` left all eighty payment database tests green** —
every one of them counted lines, entries and balances, and not one asserted which accounts the
lines land on. The trial balance cannot see it either: both are operational accounts, so the
books still balance. The row was written only after the probe was added — each line as
`DIRECTION:PURPOSE`, on the capture and on the refund's inverse pair — and the mutation then
failed naming the wrong account. Recorded here because the lesson generalises past this
invariant: **a posting test that counts lines proves the entry exists, not that it is the
right entry**, and every future money path should assert the accounts by purpose.

**Two invariants are enforced by the type system, and a mutation is a compile error.**
`INV-MON-02`'s domain half — there is no no-currency constructor — and `INV-EVT-03`'s
all-fields-mandatory construction cannot be broken at run time, so the register records the
half that *is* mutable (the schema constraint, the field set). `P0-TSK-012` handled the same
situation by invoking `javac` on the substitution; that technique is available when a compile-time
property is worth demonstrating directly.

**A cache has two directions, and one test cannot see both.** `INV-CNS-03`'s demonstration catches a gate that remembers a **permission** - that is the direction the invariant forbids, because it keeps a withdrawn basis alive. A gate that remembered only **refusals** was probed and **survived** it, correctly: withdrawal still takes effect immediately, and what goes stale is a refusal, so nothing `INV-CNS-03` says is violated. The symmetric defect is real all the same - a person who has just granted stays blocked - and it is caught at the other door, by `RegistrationOpensCaseKafkaTest#theGateHoldsTheEagerDoorAndAConsentedOpenLands`, whose second half opens a case for a party that was refused moments earlier. Recorded here because neither test covers the property alone, and a reader of either one would reasonably assume it did (`P2-TST-002`).

**A general test is not automatically the protecting one.** `INV-MON-05`'s mutation is *not* caught
by `roundTripsEveryCurrencyScale`, which writes amounts whose scale already matches the currency's
current minor units — so re-deriving gives the same answer and the test passes. Only
`storedScaleSurvivesIndependentlyOfCurrentCurrencyData` fails. Naming the *method* rather than the
class is therefore part of the convention wherever one method carries the property.

**`INV-LIFE-01` names seven operations and Phase 4 delivers one.** Its statement is *transfers,
payments, refunds, disputes, settlements, loans and agreements*, so its row records the **transfer's**
machine and nothing more; the other six arrive with Phases 5, 7, 8 and 11 and each owes its own
demonstration. The same applies to `INV-LIFE-02` and `INV-LIFE-04`, whose subject is *an operation*.
`transfers.beneficiary` has a real two-state machine with its own aggregate check and its own
every-writer trigger (`BeneficiaryTest`, `V003`), demonstrated by `P4-TSK-006`'s sweep - and it is
**deliberately not claimed** under any `INV-LIFE` row, because a saved destination is not a
money-moving operation and a row that reads stronger than it is would be the false row this register
refuses.

**The payout destination's other controls are demonstrated and deliberately not claimed**
(`P6-TSK-011`). This is the beneficiary precedent above, applied to its payout counterpart. A
payout destination decides where money goes, but it is not a money-moving operation. So its
machine, its cooling-off and its one-open and one-effective indexes are claimed under no
`INV-LIFE` or `INV-CON` row. Its bank-detail boundary is claimed under no `INV-PAY-02` row
either: a bank reference is not card data, and ADR-0056 §7 borrows that invariant's mechanism,
not its subject.

Their probes were performed all the same, and every one was caught. The catchers are in
`PayoutDestinationDatabaseTest` unless another class is named:
- the cooling-off removed from every Java layer and the constraint: caught by
  `theSchemaRefusesAnEarlyEffect` ALONE. The timeline test passed, because the sweep's candidate
  query still filtered on the deadline, a fifth layer the design had not counted. Removing that
  too failed the timeline test, `aDispatchDuringTheCoolingOffUsesThePriorDestination`, as the
  acceptance clause requires, and six more of the suite's twelve with it;
- the effect-after-deadline CHECK alone: `theSchemaRefusesAnEarlyEffect`;
- the one-open index made non-unique: `concurrentProposalsLeaveOneOpenChange` (the ten-way
  proposal race opens more than one change) and `theSchemaRefusesWhatTheDomainRefuses`;
- the supersession skipped: `aDispatchDuringTheCoolingOffUsesThePriorDestination` and
  `concurrentSweepsLeaveOneEffectiveRow` (the one-effective index then refuses the new
  destination, so it never takes effect);
- `APPROVED → WITHDRAWN` removed from the machine:
  `PayoutDestinationTest#withdrawalDuringTheCoolingOff` and
  `aWithdrawalDuringTheCoolingOffStopsTheChange`;
- the international-account shape rule removed: `BankDetailShapesTest#bankDetailsAreRefused`,
  and over HTTP `PayoutDestinationEndpointDatabaseTest#bankDetailsNeverEnter` (an account-shaped
  grant is no longer refused before the exchange) and `#tokenisationRefusalsWriteNothing` (an
  account-shaped reference from the provider is no longer refused as the owed 503);
- `V006`'s account-shape CHECK (`payout_destination_reference_is_not_an_account`) removed. This is
  the schema rank, which the bullet above does not reach, performed by `X-TSK-005`. It is caught
  by `theSchemaRefusesWhatTheDomainRefuses` at the IBAN refusal, and deterministically only since
  `X-TSK-005`. The fixture had stamped `proposed_at` with the database's `now()` and `ended_at`
  with the JVM's, so whenever the database led, `ended_at >= proposed_at` refused the same row with
  a `23514` of its own. The same removal under the old fixture **survived** with the database 5 s
  ahead, and was caught with it 0.5 s ahead over a live lag: the clock's phase decided.

ADR-0056's own invariant list claimed `INV-LIFE-01/-02/-04` until this gate, and was corrected
to match.

**The payout's two ordering locks are demonstrated and deliberately not claimed** (`P6-TSK-012`).
ADR-0057 §7's share lock on the effective destination and §11's lock on the merchant row order a
payout against a supersession and against a suspension. Neither is the rule itself. `V007`'s
insert trigger refuses, for every writer, a payout to a destination that is no longer
`EFFECTIVE`; and no catalogued invariant speaks of suspension, which is the merchant machine's
own rule. So neither lock is claimed under an `INV-CON` row. What each buys is an ordering:
- without the share lock, a payout racing a supersession is no longer committed to the
  destination effective at its commit; the trigger refuses it as a storage failure instead;
- without the merchant lock, a suspension lands between the dispatch's check and its commit, and
  a payout commits after the merchant stopped trading.

Their probes were performed all the same, and each was caught by the one test written for it,
the loser observed Lock-waiting in `pg_stat_activity` before the winner committed:
- `FOR SHARE` removed from `JdbcPayoutDestinationStore.findEffectiveForShare`:
  `MerchantPayoutDatabaseTest#aSupersessionWaitsForTheDispatch`;
- the dispatch reading the merchant with `findById` instead of `findByIdForUpdate`:
  `MerchantPayoutDatabaseTest#aSuspensionWaitsForTheDispatch`.

Neither test existed before `P6-TSK-012`'s gate, so both sections' claims were untested until then.

**The meters' other controls are demonstrated and deliberately not claimed** (`P6-TSK-013`).
Counting only acting judgements, a floor on a gauge's refresh, eager registration and a dashboard
that resolves are observability disciplines, not catalogued invariants, so none is claimed under an
`INV-*` row. Each was probed all the same, and each was caught by the test named:
- acting-only at the HTTP door - every initiation counted, or the acting bit dropped inside
  `MerchantPayouts`: `MerchantPayoutEndpointDatabaseTest#aMerchantPaysOutOverHttp` and `#aTimeoutIsHonest`;
- acting-only at the sweep - every applied answer reported as a judgement:
  `MerchantPayoutDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentSweepsLeaveOneOutcome` (ten completions where one is owed)
  and `#neverReceivedWaitsForThePermit`;
- the schedule's count of that tally removed: `MerchantPayoutDatabaseTest#theScheduleCountsActingJudgementsOnce`;
- a throwing tick rethrown, which ends a fixed-delay executor:
  `MerchantPayoutResolutionScheduleTest#aThrowingTickDoesNotEndResolution`;
- `DISPATCHED` counted as a judgement: `MerchantMetersTest`, both tests;
- the refresh floor removed: `MerchantPayoutMetricsTest#theRefreshFloorHolds`;
- the gauge bean unregistered, and the age gauge's plan row removed:
  `PlannedMetersExistTest#phase6PlannedMetersAreAlreadyPublished`;
- a wallet top-up counted as a fee assessment: `MerchantCaptureDatabaseTest#aWalletTopUpIsUnchanged`;
- every conversion recorded in one series: `CheckoutMetersTest#aConversionIsCountedAndTimed` and
  `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aCaptureLandingAfterExpiryStillProducesTheOrder`;
- the conversion age measured backwards at the completion site:
  `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentConfirmationsProduceOneOrder` (the age read zero);
- the zero clamp removed, and a completion counted without its age: `CheckoutMetersTest`;
- one of the new row's queries naming a series nobody publishes: `DashboardQueriesResolveTest`.

Three of these tests did not exist before `P6-TSK-013`'s gate: the schedule's lifecycle (missing
since `P6-TSK-012`), the running instance's gauge reading, and the conversion age's value at the
real completion site, where a count alone had been asserted.

**What the tenancy battery and the fee batch do not claim** (`P6-TST-001`):
- **The `SUBJECT` kind is classified, not probed.** An operator route naming one merchant has no
  second tenant to confuse it with. The operator population acts across tenants by permission,
  so the permission is the control, and its negatives live in each route's own suite. The battery
  holds only that every such route declares one.
- **The fingerprint is bounded.** It covers every row a merchant route can write in the
  `merchant` and `checkout` schemas, the payable's lines and holds, and the audit and outbox rows
  naming any of it. A route that wrote elsewhere would be outside it; none does today. Its
  sensitivity is proved, not assumed: every successful writing control must move it.
- **The withdrawal's own tenant read is masked over HTTP.** `P6-TSK-008`'s finding, re-performed: the withdrawal's own `findOwnedBy` removed survives the battery, because the render's owner-scoped read shares the transaction and rolls the withdrawal back. The command-level `theWithdrawalCommandIsTenantScoped` catches it. The battery is the HTTP rank, and that test is the command's.
- **The tenant's ledger rows are outside the detector.** A merchant's payable is reached by
  `owner_ref`, a column the ledger classifies for every owner alike, and the widened detector does
  not see it (`X-TSK-002`). The battery's derived reads cover it behaviourally.
- **The ledger batch is two-minor-unit only**, because the chart is. The 0- and 3-minor
  arithmetic rests on `FeeCalculationTest#theSplitConservesExactly` (JPY, USD and BHD; 7,200
  cases).
- **The residual alone cannot see a balanced mis-split.** A balanced mis-split - one unit on both fee lines of every entry - leaves the residual at zero by arithmetic. The batch catches it because it checks the books against a formula computed independently of `FeeCalculation`, not only against themselves.

**What the merchant conservation storm does not claim** (`P6-TST-002`):
- **Ambiguity under load is not its subject.** Provider timeouts are generous, so no payout or
  refund goes `UNKNOWN`. One that did would fail the tally's *nothing left in flight* rather than
  be absorbed, but resolving ambiguity under load is `P5-TST-001`'s demonstration and the payout
  resolution suites'.
- **Ten movers share one JVM.** Each command has its own pooled connection, so the movers contend
  in the database as ten instances would. The beans they share hold no correctness state, which
  `NoSingleInstanceAssumptionRulesTest` enforces. Two application instances in two JVMs are not
  run.
- **Its arithmetic is narrow on purpose.** Every sale is 10.00 so each fee and fee share is exact,
  and the breadth of amounts, rates and rounding policies is `P6-TST-001`'s fee batch.
- **One repricing, one direction.** Each merchant's price rises once, half-way, with refunds in
  flight across it. A price that falls is not driven, here or in the single-flow `INV-MER-03`
  tests, which reprice upwards too.


**What the Phase 6 review's corrections do not claim** (`P6-DOC-001`):
- **The route register pins declarations, not behaviour per route.** `RoutePermissionRegisterTest`
  proves each route requires exactly its registered permission, and `DenyByDefaultDatabaseTest`
  proves the interceptor refuses whoever lacks a declared one. The two compose into each route's
  negative; the review did not add thirty-two behavioural negatives on top.
- **The fee pin's count is not a demonstration of its key.** Ten concurrent confirmations leave
  one pin, but the claim and the session's conditional move serialise the racers before any
  reaches the pin, so removing the pin's primary key would not be caught there. The key stands as
  the last rank behind them; the pin's own convergence and refusal are
  `MerchantCaptureDatabaseTest#aSecondPinAtADifferentPriceIsRefused`'s.
- **The share-locked destination read's one-statement blind spot is analysed, not driven.**
  ADR-0056 §5 states the fail-safe refusal; no lock-choreographed test produces it.
- **The confined-credential guard pins names, not deployments.** It proves each refusal names the
  variable its property binds; it does not start the application off loopback.

**What the Phase 6 → 7 transition's repairs do not claim** (thirty-three probes, all caught, every
restore verified byte-identical):
- **The convergence on a payment that succeeded meanwhile is analysed, not driven.** A
  confirmation that reads `PAYMENT_PENDING` and then meets `payments.NotConfirmable` because the
  capture landed in between renders the paid session; producing that interleaving needs the
  capture to commit inside one HTTP call's gap, and no lock stands in it to choreograph. The
  ten-racer storm reaches it by chance, and asserts only that no racer is told the session left
  the flow.
- **The instrument refusal is a mapping, not an invariant.** `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#aForeignInstrumentIsRefusedAndWritesNothing`
  was probed (the catch removed: a `500`) and is recorded here rather than in §2, because a
  server fault answering a client error breaks the error contract, not a financial property.
- **The send permit's two ranks are each proven, but the ranks beneath them are not re-broken.**
  `V009`'s clauses are driven by raw SQL and the command's judgement by the three permit tests;
  `V008`'s unique dispatch key, which caught the takeover's fresh dispatch before the transition,
  now stands behind a convergence that finds the row first, and was not probed again.
- **The stored-rail read is the shape, not yet a falsifiable claim** (`P7-TSK-001`). The
  clearing purpose is resolved from the rail the attempt ROW stores, never from the resolving
  instance's wiring — but with one declared rail the two cannot disagree, so no mutation can
  tell them apart yet. The second rail (`P7-TSK-006`) makes the distinction falsifiable, and
  the routing storm inherits it.
- **The trigger's model keys, the sweep queries' model filters and the store's writer-side
  guard are second-rank** (`P7-TSK-002`). With non-terminal vocabularies model-exclusive —
  pinned structurally — a model-blind union of the per-model edge lists is extensionally
  identical on coherent rows, the card sweep's status lists already exclude every push state,
  and `anyPermits` equals the owning model's own `permits`. The falsifiable ranks, both probed,
  are the vocabulary `CHECK` and each model's own edge list; the model keys become
  independently falsifiable the day a status list or an edge set stops being model-exclusive —
  which is exactly the day they are needed.
- **The push and book aggregate rank is constructor coherence plus door refusals**
  (`P7-TSK-002`). No push or book operation exists yet, so their aggregate layer is proven as:
  the coherent foreign row constructs, every existing door refuses it, no foreign fact rides
  it. Their own doors' demonstrations arrive with their rails (`P7-TSK-006`, `-009`,
  `-011`).
- **A substring pin cannot see a comment-out that preserves the statement's text**
  (`P7-TSK-002`). The backfill probe's first form — the `UPDATE` behind `--` with its bytes
  intact — passed the migration test's `.contains(...)` pin; only the text-changing form
  (`WHERE false`, the `V011` precedent) fails it. Disarm probes use text-changing forms for
  that recorded reason.
- **The in-force tie-break is not yet falsifiable** (`P7-TSK-003`). Newest-`effective_from`
  beats highest-number only when two effective versions overlap, and every version the current
  operator flow creates is effective immediately or scheduled forward without overlap, so the
  ORDER-only mutation is extensionally harmless while the `WHERE` stands — the analysed
  survivor. It becomes falsifiable the first time a scheduled version coexists with a later
  immediate one; the probe that matters today — effectiveness ignored outright — is run in its
  falsifiable form and caught.
- **The refund's acting-only audit was not probed on its own.** Ten sweepers resolving one refund
  are counted to one completion and one entry (`tenConcurrentSweepsResolveOneRefundOnce`); the
  refund's no-audit branch for a losing resolver is `applyRefund`'s own, beside the attempt's in
  `PaymentOutcomes.answered`, and only the attempt's was broken on purpose
  (`concurrentSweepersRaceToOneWinner`).
- **`INV-SET-01` at the clearing recorder is structural, not mutational** (`P7-TSK-005`). `PaymentClearing`'s dependency list contains no ledger, no outcome component and no machine door, so no single-fragment mutation of shipped code can make a clearing post or transition; the behavioural pins stand in front of the structure — every clearing test asserts the journal count and the attempt's state byte-unchanged, including on the unconcluded attempt a racing notice attached to. The falsifiable rank arrives with Phase 8's settlement matching, where a posting exists to protect.

**The rail and dispute meters' other controls are demonstrated and deliberately not claimed**
(`P7-TSK-015`). Counting each acting judgement exactly once where it is written, after its commit,
the rail series following the declarations, the stage gauge and the report's arithmetic are
observability and reporting disciplines, not catalogued invariants, so none is claimed under an
`INV-*` row. Each was probed all the same, and each was caught by the test named:
- each applier's report removed - attempts: `PaymentEndpointDatabaseTest#theAcceptanceChainHolds`,
  `#cancellingAnAuthorizedPaymentReleasesThePromise` and `PayByBankDatabaseTest#aLostCallbackIsResolvedByInquiry`;
  refunds: `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentRefundWebhooksProduceOneEffect` and
  `PayByBankDatabaseTest#aLostReturnAnswerResolvesBySweep`; withdrawals:
  `WithdrawalDatabaseTest#aLostAnswerResolvesByInquiryToOneEntry`; dispute answers:
  `DisputeResponseDatabaseTest#aRepresentmentIsSubmittedAndWonEndToEnd` and
  `#aLostAnswerIsUnknownUntilTheSweepResolvesIt`; the book payment:
  `CheckoutFlowDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentWalletConfirmsProduceOneEntry`;
- a CONVERGED application reported as a judgement - refunds:
  `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest#aConvergedRefundApplicationCountsNothing`; attempts:
  `PaymentSweeperDatabaseTest#aConvergedCaptureIsCountedOnce`. **Neither test existed before the
  gate, and the first probe survived**: it was aimed at the ten-webhook race, whose nine losers no
  longer reach the refund applier at all - the door's locked read (the Phase 6 → 7 transition)
  finds the refund finished and applies nothing, so that test's own comment still credited an
  acting bit it can no longer exercise (corrected). The one converged refund application left
  reachable is an ambiguous answer on a row another resolver already moved into `UNKNOWN`; the
  attempt's is the sweep's unlocked read, which the ten-sweeper race reaches nine times but through
  `RailOutcomeObserver.NONE`. Both now run deterministically over a registry the test owns, and
  both probes were re-run and caught;
- a judgement writer called outside its applier: `JudgementWritersAreConfinedTest#everyJudgementWriterCallIsItsAppliers`;
- counted before the commit: `CommittedRailOutcomesTest#aJudgementCountsOnlyOnceItsTransactionCommits`
  and `#aRolledBackJudgementCountsNothing`;
- a failing meter rethrown into a committed operation: `CommittedRailOutcomesTest#aFailingMeterNeverPropagates`;
- `AWAITING_PAYER` counted as a judgement: `CommittedRailOutcomesTest#aMidQuestionStatusCountsNothing`;
- `VOID_UNKNOWN` left uncounted: `CommittedRailOutcomesTest#everyUnknownIsAJudgement`;
- the withdrawal series registered on every rail rather than the push rails that declare it:
  `PaymentMetersTest#theRailSeriesFollowTheDeclarations`;
- the push rail timed under the card PSP's name again - the mislabel this task found, restored:
  `PaymentMetersTest#thePushRailIsTimedUnderItsOwnName`;
- the dispute port timed only when it answers: `PaymentMetersTest#theDisputePortIsTimed`;
- every dispute counted at one stage: `DisputeResponseDatabaseTest#aRepresentmentIsSubmittedAndWonEndToEnd`;
- the stage gauge's unreadable reading published as zeros: `DisputeStageMetricsTest#unreadableIsNaNNeverZero`;
- the report counting a wallet as a merchant, the window's end made inclusive, a chargeback dated
  by the dispute's opening rather than its escalation, inquiries counted as chargebacks, and an
  undefined ratio not ranked first: `ChargebackRatioReportDatabaseTest#theMonthIsCountedAndRanked`,
  five probes;
- the report unbounded: `ChargebackRatioReportDatabaseTest#theReportIsBounded`;
- the rail filter dropped: `ChargebackRatioReportDatabaseTest#theRailFilterIsTheCallersData`;
- the reportable range unbounded (a pre-2000 month, a future one, `9999-12`):
  `ChargebackRatioReportDatabaseTest#theReportIsGuarded`.

The range's refusal and the wired stage gauge's reading were also the gate's own additions: the
report first accepted any four-digit year, `9999-12` pushing its window's end into year 10000, and
no test read the running instance's stage gauge against the real schema.

**What the multi-rail conservation storm does not claim** (`P7-TST-001`):
- **The trial balance reading is not independently falsifiable.** Every entry balances by the
  journal's own constraint (`INV-LED-01`'s ranks), so no application mutation can unbalance a
  currency; the storm reads `INV-ACC-01` to prove it stays zero under three rails' traffic, and
  its probes break what the trial balance cannot see - a posting on the wrong rail's position, a
  posting missing, a hold left standing.
- **The meters' tally is demonstrated and deliberately not claimed.** A withdrawal's judgement left
  uncounted was caught at rest - the tally found both withdrawal outcomes missing from the counts
  the tables committed - but counting is an observability discipline, not a catalogued invariant.
- **It chooses only three losses.** Captures, withdrawals and pay-ins are lost on purpose; a
  refund's or a return's answer lost to a transport failure under load is resolved exactly the
  same way - the providers answer by reference, truthfully - and counted, never forbidden. The
  harness's first form answered only the chosen losses, and an honest `UNKNOWN` refund stood at
  rest.
- **Voids and payouts stay outside it.** A void moves no money and races ten ways in its own suite
  (`PaymentVoidDatabaseTest#tenRacersSettleOneTerminalFamily`); payouts were `P6-TST-002`'s
  storm.
- **Parked shares, representments and second-cycle chargebacks are `P7-TST-002`'s.** No storm
  account closes, so no share is parked; the reconciliation's arithmetic carries the parked term
  all the same.
- **The deadlock's storm verdict is a race's.** The storm met the win-against-refund deadlock
  once in five runs while it was being built; the chargeback suite's race is its deterministic
  catcher, and the storm run beside that probe passed.
- **Ten movers share one JVM** - `P6-TST-002`'s note, standing: each command has its own pooled
  connection, the shared beans hold no correctness state, two application instances in two JVMs
  are not run.

**What the dispute battery does not claim** (`P7-TST-002`):
- **Its counterparties are customer wallets.** The split is judged in `payments` whoever holds
  the account, and a merchant's payable enters a dispute only through
  `MerchantBoundDisputeComposition`'s two lines - which the multi-rail storm drives under load on
  its sales (`P7-TST-001`) and the single-flow suite names in the payable's drill-down.
- **Several probes are caught at the schema rank, not by a reading.** The split blind to refunds or
  to standing shares, the refund's bound blind to chargebacks, parking disabled and the fee guard
  dropped each meet the rank standing behind the domain's - `V021`'s triggers, `V007`, the fee's
  conditional - and the battery absorbs no non-domain outcome, so the break surfaces as a 500 at the
  door or a storage failure from the refund. The "at both ranks" probes are the battery's own
  readings catching what no rank stops - and the refund's bound dropped at BOTH of its ranks was
  still caught by a THIRD, the dispute's own attribution trigger judging the same arithmetic at the
  next stage transition.
- **The refund's side of the bound is forced, not raced.** In-process refunds reach the attempt lock
  before the door's HTTP deliveries in most runs; the chargeback's side is the race
  (`REFUND_RACING#0`), the refund's side a partial chargeback standing first (`REFUND_RACING#1`).
- **The inbox's contended redelivery is a safety net no observed run needed.** The bounded claim
  wait absorbed every identical-event-id race; only `api.Conflict` would be redelivered.
- **The lock order's verdict is a race's.** With rounds (four, then eight per instance) the battery
  met the win-against-refunds deadlock itself in three runs of seven - a race cannot be made certain,
  and the chargeback suite's race, which caught it in every run it joined, remains its
  deterministic catcher.
- **Headroom freed after the fact is outside it.** No scenario lands a capture late, fails a counted
  refund or wins a sibling with excess standing - the re-attribution record is asserted ABSENT on
  every dispute; `ChargebackAccountingDatabaseTest` carries all three.
- **The Phase 7 review's probes (`P7-DOC-001`) and what it tested without one.** Twenty-four
  runs over twenty-three breaks, twenty-three caught at the first run; the survivor (D22) was a
  test weaker than its name - the push machine refuses a void the capability gate no longer does,
  and a bare 409 could not tell the ranks apart - tightened and caught. Recorded without a probe:
  the ten-way sweeps for withdrawals, pay-in initiations and returns and the instant callbacks'
  effect counts sit behind the row lock, the conditional transition, the posting key and the
  aggregate's guard, each probed at its own task, so a single break is absorbed by the next rank;
  these tests demonstrate the composition. The availability change after a decision is a property
  of absence - no path reroutes, so no single edit makes one. The chain walks are joins over
  stored identifiers frozen by trigger.
- **Tenancy and encryption are not re-proven by new code.** "The tenancy rows re-run" is
  `MerchantTenancyBatteryDatabaseTest` and `DisputeResponseDatabaseTest` inside the gate's battery;
  the battery's own `INV-DSP-03` share is the answers' transmission and dispatch records under a
  race, and a refused act's absence from the record.

---

## 4. The register — `P{n}-TST-*` items

The task's own acceptance criterion: the convention is applied to every `P0-TST-*` item.

| Item | Its criterion's mutation | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| `P0-TST-001` | Break rounding, currency checking or overflow handling | All three named breaks fail. A later sweep found three surviving mutants and closed two real gaps — a reversed `compareTo`, and `equals` ignoring scale |
| `P0-TST-002` | Change the allocator to naive division | The sweep fails. Both sweeps also assert they encountered indivisible remainders, so neither can pass by allocating only divisible amounts |
| `P0-TST-003` | Remove propagation from a sink | The test fails, with a negative control proving an unwrapped handoff loses the identifier |
| `P0-TST-004` | Drop the unique constraint | 17 tests fail |
| `P0-TST-005` | Move the outbox write outside the business transaction | Three tests fail |
| `P0-TST-006` | Drop the inbox primary key | Nine tests fail across three classes |
| `P0-TST-007` | Widen the privilege grant | Six tests at table level, two at column level |
| `P0-TST-008` | Add a sensitive field without redaction | The rule rejects it — after `P0-TST-008` fixed the rule, which could not fail at all |
| `P0-TST-009` | Revert the `V004` fix so the lease is judged by the client's clock | The corrected skew test fails. Before the correction it stayed green, which is the defect that task found |

---
| `P1-TST-001` | Plant an unwrapped `String lastPassword` in real production code; separately, narrow the unwrap whitelist | The build fails twice for the field - once for the component, once for the accessor - and the whitelist rejects a new `expose()` anywhere. `P1-TSK-009` also found the rule's vocabulary was **one third structurally unreachable**, and a `cardNumber` field passed cleanly before the fix |
| `P1-TST-002` | Make `supersede` unconditional; separately, make the failure-counter predicate unsatisfiable | Caught only after the test was rewritten to assert the **coordination** rather than the outcome - three mechanisms produce the same end state, and an outcome-only assertion cannot name which one did (`P1-TSK-012`) |
| `P1-TST-003` | Add an un-enumerated session-issuing path; separately, turn `atLeast` into equality | The enumeration guard fails on the first, and `assuranceIsALevelAndNotABoolean` on the second. The suite was written after a probe found a **real bypass**: a `PASSWORD` session could begin a replacement enrolment with an attacker-controlled secret (`P1-TSK-019`) |
| `P2-TST-001` | Reopen a terminal case state or drop the aggregate's transition check; map a provider verdict onto success; deliver one callback three times and ten ways concurrently | The cross-product sweep (`KycCaseLifecycleTest#everyTransitionIsEnforced`) fails on both lifecycle mutations (`P2-TSK-005`); the adapter matrix fails the mapping (`P2-TSK-009`); the duplicate tests fail when either dedupe layer is bypassed, and one decision survives the ten-way race (`P2-TSK-011`, `P2-TSK-013`). The item's own deliverable is the five `INV-KYC` rows in §2, which the register guard begins demanding the day Phase 2's status flips `COMPLETE` |
| `P2-TST-002` | Replace the gate's authoritative read with a cached value | `ConsentGateDatabaseTest#withdrawalOnOneInstanceRefusesOnAnother` fails, for a gate-level cache and for one hidden a layer down in the store. **Performing it changed the test**: written with one `ConsentGate` shared by both simulated instances it caught the mutation by accident (A memoises before B withdraws), and `SimulatedInstance`'s own rule - never share the thing whose sharing hides the defect - demanded a gate per instance. Moving to that revealed the test had been resting on autocommit, and it now straddles the **commit** instead: uncommitted, A still permits; committed, A's next decision refuses. Two further probes bound the claim - the field detector survives a store-level cache, and a refusal-only cache survives this test and is caught by the kafka door. Reading the item's own words also found the demonstration aimed one level too low: it proved the **gate** flipped and left the **capability** to a composition argument, so `ConsentWithdrawalBlocksTheCapabilityDatabaseTest` drives the real consumer and asserts no case, no audit record and no announcement - with a positive control, so a capability that opened nothing ever could not pass it |
| `P3-TST-001` | Update the projection outside the posting transaction | Five tests fail across `BalanceProjectionDatabaseTest` and `SustainedConcurrentPostingDatabaseTest`, the two intended assertions among them - the rolled-back posting deterministically, and the sustained storm's drift-free-throughout sweep. The item's own deliverable is the `INV-BAL-02` row in section 2 and the sustained composition itself: the storm is ended by the VERIFIER (sweeps and committed-entry floors both met before it stops the posters), so the overlap is a property of the loop's exit condition rather than of scheduling luck, and the final row is held to the posters' own committed tally, tracked outside the kernel. The rebuild half records the operator procedure that is safe under live posting - lock-then-look, because a blocked `UPDATE` re-evaluates recomputing subqueries against its ORIGINAL snapshot (`P2-TSK-015`'s finding) and would lose exactly the posting it blocked on |
| `P3-TST-002` | Remove the account row's `FOR UPDATE`; move the availability check's inputs outside the lock; read the holds from the projection instead of the hold rows | The removed lock fails the deterministic interleaving's blocked-observation precondition; the moved check keeps that precondition green and fails the outcome half plus the ten-way count - **performed by this item**, because `P3-TSK-015`'s sweep had performed the check *dropped* and moved-outside is the different defect that still locks, still checks and still loses the race; the projection read fails the corrupted-`holds_minor` test. The item's own deliverable is the `INV-CON-01` and `INV-BAL-04` rows in section 2; `INV-BAL-05`'s row is **deferred in writing to the exit review** (the `P2-TST-001` handling of `INV-KYC-06`), its demonstration already performed and recorded inside the `INV-BAL-04` row |
| `P4-TST-001` | Take the two participants' row locks in no fixed order (the source alone); derive availability before the lock grant; post one minor unit less than the transfer records | **The demonstration found a real defect, which is what a composition item is for.** Ten instances moving money both ways between one pair produced **783 deadlocks against 203 domain outcomes** - the `journal_line` foreign key takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the destination (`P3-TSK-014`'s mechanism) while the execution held `FOR UPDATE` on the source, so A->B and B->A are a cycle. The one-directional drain could not reach it, because a cycle needs two directions, and the method's own javadoc had said for three tasks that *the destination is deliberately never locked* - true of the explicit lock, false about what happens. Remedied with the idiom the sibling store method already names (`lockOwnedForUpdate` orders by id *so two multi-account closers cannot deadlock*), and the storm went from 206s and 79% aborts to 4.5s and none. **A surviving mutation then corrected the test**: the pre-lock availability derivation survived the first draft, because amounts of 1.00-3.00 against 10.00 never bring an account near zero and a stale read only differs at the boundary - with 7.00 and 9.00 in the rotation it is caught mid-storm at -5.00. The item's own deliverable is the `INV-CON-02` row in section 2; the other four `Phase: 4` rows are `P4-TST-002`'s, read from the catalogue |
| `P4-TST-002` | Substitute a pre-flight read for the unique constraint in the idempotency claim, so a duplicate is decided by a `SELECT` that cannot see an uncommitted sibling | **The audit is the deliverable, and it found the gap it was written to find.** The `Phase: 4` set read token-exactly from the catalogue is **five** - `INV-IDEM-01` (transfers), `INV-CON-02` (landed by `P4-TST-001`) and `INV-LIFE-01/-02/-04` - and the four remaining rows are here. Two findings. **The guard would never have demanded the `INV-IDEM-01` row**: it keys on the invariant identifier and that identifier already carries the kernel and posting rows, so the transfers-context row is owed by `PHASE_GATES.md` section Phase 4 (*the transfers-context row included*) and by doctrine - the existing rows prove the mechanism, not this caller - and by nothing the build can say. **And the caller had no concurrent-duplicate test at all**, while the catalogue's own Verify line reads *concurrent-duplicate integration tests* and the phase's criterion 2 reads *proven under concurrent submission from two threads*: what existed was the sequential retry, which exercises the **replay** path because by then the record is committed. Writing the row against it would have been the false row this register refuses, so the demonstration was **performed** (the `P3-TST-002` precedent: the audit finds what was not done and does it) - `TransferExecutionDatabaseTest#tenConcurrentIdenticalKeysProduceOneTransfer`, ten instances on one key, one effect counted in four tables. The mutation leaves **every sequential test green** and fails exactly the new one, which is what makes it load-bearing rather than a second copy of `P0-TST-004`. One documentation finding recorded on the way: section 6 item 4 claimed the class-existence check covers *either register*, and it reads section 2 only |
| `P5-TST-001` | Map a provider timeout to `FAILED` before the ambiguity fold; make the sweeper's transition unconditional; fold the wire client's 404 into the explicit `UNRECOGNISED` licence | Eight, all ended caught - **and the 404 fold SURVIVED its first run**, masked by the probe's empty-bodied 404 dying at the evidence bound before the status-code fold could matter. Strengthened to the bodied 404 a misrouted load balancer sends, the mutation then failed. The row for this item landed with `P5-TST-002`, which found it missing: the demonstrations were performed at the owning gate, the §4 entry was not written |
| `P5-TST-002` | The audit-not-invent sweep over every `Phase: 5` invariant, the set read from the **catalogue** with the guard's own token regex; the capture's debit pointed at a suspense account; one register method reference corrupted; the flip simulated and one new row removed | **The account mutation SURVIVED the entire payment database tier - 80 tests green while a customer's wallet was funded from `SUSPENSE_UNMATCHED`** - because every test counted lines and none asserted which accounts they land on. `INV-SET-01` therefore had no test that could fail, and the row was written only after the probe was added (`DIRECTION:PURPOSE` per line, on the capture and the refund's inverse pair); the mutation then failed naming the wrong account. §5 teeth re-proven by the corrupted reference; the flip probed and both files restored byte-identical |
| `P5-TST-003` | Release a refund's hold without posting; drop the availability check from `HoldService.place`; transition a capture without its posting; **invert the refund's pinned attempt → account lock order**; reduce the bound's lock-then-look to a plain read | Five, and **the fourth SURVIVED - which is the whole value of this item**. Conservation caught the unposted release (140.00 the refund rows said had left, still in wallets); the dropped availability check drove a wallet to -3.00 mid-storm; the unposted capture was caught at the priming stage by the vacuity guard (an empty journal verifies no currency) rather than by the conservation reading - recorded as observed. **The inverted lock order passed the entire storm**, because refunders drew their subjects from a queue the capturers filled AFTER `capture()` returned, so a capture and a refund could never contend for one attempt row - the only interleaving that deadlocks. Refunders now discover attempts from the DATABASE, whatever state they are in, and the mutation then failed with SQLSTATE `40P01`. The fifth survived CORRECTLY, as `P5-TSK-015`'s row already records: the account lock serialises the holds and the advisory-locked trigger is the third layer, so the funded-wallet race is that claim's own probe. Two further findings were live failing runs rather than planted mutations: polite refund amounts left the availability boundary uncontested through four rewrites (the `P4-TST-001` lesson, arriving anyway), and a spender without the account lock drove a wallet negative - the storm finding its own defect, since a posting is deliberately not availability-checked |
| `P3-TST-003` | The audit-not-invent sweep over every `Phase: 3` invariant, with the set read from the **catalogue** and not the plan | **The set is nineteen, and the plan's own section 6 table lists seventeen** — `INV-REC-05` and `INV-AUD-04` sit outside it, the exact drift the item's scope predicted (`INV-HIST-02`'s class; the plan's closing paragraph already rules the catalogue wins, so the finding is recorded rather than the table edited). Fourteen rows, two extension rows and a financial-boundary `INV-IDEM-01` row landed — seventeen, counted — every named mutation one its owning task **performed** — the `P2-TST-001` posture, record rather than re-perform — and every named class and method held to the code by this register's guard from the moment the rows landed. `INV-BAL-05`'s row lands here, earlier than `P3-TST-002`'s recorded deferral, because this item's scope demands every row and what the deferral postponed was the record, not the work. **The sixteenth row cannot be written**: `INV-AUD-04`'s mechanism is deliberately unbuilt (section 3), so the item's deliverable there is the finding, the section-3 entry, and `P3-TSK-021` created to land the mechanism and its row before `P3-DOC-001`. The F1–F8 assessment itself is `docs/project/reviews/PHASE_3_FINANCIAL_SUPPLEMENT.md`, each criterion held to named tests the register also names |
| `P6-TST-001` | Neutralise each addressed statement's tenant predicate; add a route the table does not name; break the fee arithmetic so that every entry still balances | **31 probes, 29 caught.** Every addressed predicate neutralised was caught, and each failure message is the leak itself: A read B's session and payout, approved B's payout destination, revoked B's key. Two new routes and an identifier parameter each failed the route table without a test written for them. The fee batch caught a defaulted rounding policy, a stored policy ignored at read, and a BALANCED mis-split - one unit on both fee lines - that the residual alone cannot see; the independent formula is what sees it. **The two survivors are recorded, not hidden.** A keys-blind fingerprint still moved on a revocation's event row and audit record, and a fingerprint returning a constant was then caught by the sensitivity check alone. The withdrawal's own tenant read is masked over HTTP by the render's, and the command-level test caught it. Two first runs did not compile and are not counted. The dispatch's destination read turned out to have a second rank in `V007`'s own-destination trigger |
| `P6-TST-002` | Judge payouts and refunds without each other's holds; price a capture by the version in force while sales straddle a repricing; post a payout to the wrong counterpart; forget a replay's key | **Eleven probes, ten caught.** The overcommitted payable, a capture priced by the version in force, a refund priced the same way, a payout's wrong counterpart, a hold left standing and a refund replayed without its claim were each caught - the first four mid-storm, inside one snapshot per sweep round, where the storm reconciles every payable to records the ledger never sees. **The survivor is a payout's claim alone**, held by the dispatch key's schema rank; with both ranks broken the replay dispatched a second payout and was caught. **The storm's own teeth**: without the repricing it refuses to pass; without payers-out there was no `PayoutUnfunded` and not a single `RefundUnfunded` across 74 completed refunds, because the drain is what makes a refund meet an unfunded payable (`P5-TST-003`'s spender lesson, on the merchant book); and read without its snapshot, the reconciliation fails under traffic. **At the simulated flip the guard demanded exactly this row**: all nine `Phase: 6` invariants already have theirs, and `PlannedMetersExistTest`'s derived guard passes |
| `P7-TST-001` | Post each rail's completion to another rail's clearing (a withdrawal, a pay-in, a chargeback and its fee); read the storm's snapshot under `READ COMMITTED`; drop the hold's availability check; complete a withdrawal without its posting, and leaving its hold; judge a chargeback's split blind to refunds; take the win's counterparty lock away; leave a withdrawal's judgement uncounted | **Ten probes, ten caught** - every reconciliation the storm claims failed in the round the break first reached it, by its own reading (`SETTLEMENT_CLEARING` 188.50 against 212.50, `INSTANT_CLEARING` 80.00 against 56.00, a wallet at -4959.00, a hold of 12.00 with nothing in flight, a wallet read 53.00 against its books' 45.00 without the snapshot), the split's break at the schema rank behind it (a 500 the storm will not absorb), the meters' tally at rest. **The storm's own finds**: a dispute WIN deadlocked with a refund of another payment to the same counterparty (`40P01`) - the win's first entry took the clearing's balance row before its restoration touched the counterparty the refund held - now closed by share-locking the counterparty before a stage's first posting, with its own race; an honest `UNKNOWN` refund stood at rest while the harness answered only its chosen losses, now answered truthfully; and a refusal the storm counted on happening, one run in five, did not - every contested boundary is now forced by a never-affordable amount; and inside the gate's battery the tally called a burst's honestly failed payment - its capture never reached the provider - a missing order: every lost answer and every burst's order is now judged by what the provider received |
| `P7-TST-002` | Break the win's inverse by one unit; write the whole chargeback off at a loss; judge the split blind to refunds and to standing chargebacks, and the refund's bound blind to chargebacks - each at the domain rank and again at both ranks; count a won chargeback as standing; disable parking; disarm the stage guard on answers; re-enter a stage per delivery; bypass the inbox; drop the fee's first-report guard; take the win's counterparty lock away | **Fifteen probes, fifteen caught, fourteen by the battery itself** - each failure naming its scenario and the record it broke: the per-dispute reconciliation (a win's 9.99 against its record's 10.00; a loss writing off a share the counterparty bore), THE GATE'S CRITERION's own assertion (a refunded payment charged again, with both ranks of the bound gone), the second cycle's split, the record counts (twelve stage records for two stages, the trail still one row per edge), the door's tally (no duplicate absorbed where 372 were due); the domain-rank breaks met the schema rank behind them (500s at the door, a refused refund insert), and the refund's bound dropped at both of its ranks still met a third. The win's missing lock: the chargeback suite's race every time, the battery in three runs of seven. **Found while building it**: the refund race never raced - the refund's side of the bound was exercised by nothing, now forced. **The gate's own finds**: every `409` read as contention (a refused posting redelivered for a minute); the missing lock unseen with one race a run; a refused answer's absence from the record asserted nowhere |
| `P8-TST-001` | Remove a card capture's expectation; make an approved resolution's entry an unknown poster; unpark a correction one minor unit short; bypass the per-source try-lock and drop the three deferred Sigma triggers together; post every recognition to the PSP's clearing; perturb the replay's fee tolerance; drop a late leg's meter count | **Eight demonstrations, eight caught**, each on the final suite in its own container, every restore byte-identical (sha256-verified): the position proof in round 1; completeness (`line.unattributed`) after the resolutions; the position and unowned-suspense readings in round 2; the break census in round 1 **while the over-allocation readings held** (the census is the lock's, the money the schema's); `INSTANT_CLEARING` unexplained once a scheme report was accepted; a PSP run replayed `DIVERGED`; the second tally short. The backlog's "raw-SQL clearing line" and "park without its break" were performed as an unknown-poster entry and a short unpark - each makes the same reading fail. The storm's production correction (the anchored rematch clause's two clocks) was reversed alone by the gate and caught by `PayoutMatchingDatabaseTest` (n) |
| `P8-TST-002` | Shorten every posting kind's lines by one unit; remove the self-approval refusal at the domain, the `resolution` CHECK and ledger `V010` in turn; remove the stale check; drop the origin refusal; grant `DELETE`; make `V014` inert in each of its four rules and the domain's cause condition | **Thirteen probes, thirteen caught**, every restore byte-identical (sha256-verified): each four-eyes rank caught by its own assertion while the other two still refused; the gate's critical find (a reclassified diverged replay closed by one person) corrected by `V014` keyed on the break's cause and probed six ways. One first attempt broke a migration's SQL and gave no results - recorded as no verdict, re-aimed, caught |
| `P9-TST-002` | Round the customer's computed amount in one direction (a directed rounding in `ConversionPlan`); post a cover's realised loss as a gain (`CoverLines`) - each with the battery run small (`FINAPP_FX_BATTERY_CONVERSIONS=400`) | Two probes, both caught, each by its own identity - the directed rounding left no negative residual in the run (the both-signs census; the replay, using the same function, could not see it), and the loss posted as a gain broke FX_REALISED_GAINS and FX_REALISED_LOSSES in EUR (-7.44 against 0, and 0 against 7.44) in the books proof |
| `P9-TST-001` | Leave a failing credit's hold standing (`OutboundCreditOutcomes.fail`); post a conversion's margin to the rounding residual's book (`ConversionLines`); bypass the FX callback door's signature check (`FxCallbackService`) - each on the final suite in its own container | THREE STORM PROBES, THREE CAUGHT - a failing credit's hold left standing caught by the holds check in round 6, a conversion's margin on the rounding residual's book by the FX books proof in round 2, the FX callback door's signature check bypassed by the forged-callback refusal (204 where 401 was due) - run on the suite before its last two additions (the two-instance races and the at-rest census), which add readings and remove none; and the sweep's fair order reverted, caught by its regression test |

## 5. Applying this to a new test

1. Write the test.
2. Break the property it protects — in production code, in the schema, or in a fixture.
3. Run the tests. **Read the names of what failed**, not just the count: a mutation caught by an
   unrelated assertion is not a demonstration, and this repository has twice reported one as caught
   when a different test had objected.
4. Revert, and confirm the revert landed. `git checkout --` is a no-op on an untracked file and has
   three times destroyed uncommitted work on a tracked one; copy-based backup is the reliable form.
5. Record the mutation, the command and the result — here for an `INV-*`, in the change log
   otherwise.

**Isolate the mutation to the assertion under test.** `P0-TSK-031` changed two occurrences at once
and the failure came from a different guard entirely, which reported the mutation as caught when
the assertion under test had let it through.

---

## 6. What is enforced

`MutationDemonstrationTest`, on every build:

1. Every invariant `FINANCIAL_INVARIANTS.md` marks as belonging to **a phase this project has
   reached** has a register row in §2. The current phase is read from `CURRENT_STATE.md`
   (`P1-TSK-024`), so Phase 2 needs no change here.
2. Every `P{n}-TST-*` item in `BACKLOG.md` has a register row in §4 — found wherever a **bold
   heading** declares it, because Phase 0 gives those items their own headings and Phase 1 names them
   inside task headings, and anchoring to either shape passes vacuously for the other.
3. Every invariant §2 names **exists in the catalogue** — the other direction, so a row that has
   quietly stopped applying to anything is not indistinguishable from one that still does. Added
   during review, which found a planted `INV-ZZZ-99` row passing cleanly.
4. Every test class named in the **section 2** register **exists** - and only section 2.
   The claim read *either register* until `P4-TST-002` checked it: section 4's rows contribute
   their item identifier and nothing else, because that section's prose columns are full of
   backticked tokens that are not tests (`INV-CON-02`, `P3-TSK-014`, `lockOwnedForUpdate`,
   `23514`), and scanning them would fail the build on a method name quoted in an
   explanation. Widening the guard needs a reference column section 4 does not have; stating
   what is actually checked is the honest half, and it is the same *documentation describing
   behaviour that does not exist* class this register was built to catch.
5. Every method named in **any section 2 row** **exists on that class** — so a claim of continuous
   proof cannot point at a method that was renamed or deleted. This caught the `INV-IDEM-03` row
   written during review, which named a method that does not exist.
6. **Every row names at least one test the parser can read** (`P1-TSK-024`). Checks 4 and 5 are
   satisfied vacuously by a row that parses to nothing, and that is not hypothetical: the grammar
   admitted exactly one backticked reference and **nine rows did not parse**, eight of them written
   during Phase 1, so the tests they named were never checked. The check is **per row** rather than
   per invariant, because an invariant may have two rows and merging their references lets an
   unreadable one hide behind a readable sibling — established by a mutation that survived the first
   version of this very check.
7. **Every §2 line that looks like a row parses as one** (`P1-TSK-024`, completion gate). Check 6
   sees a row that parses and names nothing; it cannot see a row that fails the pattern **entirely**,
   because such a row is not in the map at all. A form column reading `Insuite` rather than
   `In-suite` left the build green, and the invariant stayed covered only by its sibling rows.
6. Both forms are present and labelled, so the form column cannot quietly stop carrying
   information.
7. The registers are actually parsed, so a reformatted table fails loudly rather than silently
   matching nothing, and the in-suite rows are asserted to have actually resolved.

**Not enforced:** that a `Recorded` demonstration still reproduces. Re-running one means mutating
production code or the schema, which a build must not do to itself. That is the residual risk the
form column exists to make visible, and the reason to prefer in-suite proofs.
